## #202 — A media query adds no specificity, and now a guard says so

- merged · opened 2026-08-30 · merged 2026-08-30
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/202>

> ## The defect, four times
>
> This has now shipped four times across the estate and no test in any of the
> three suites said a word about any of them:
>
> | selector | product |
> |---|---|
> | `.vault-light` | PDI |
> | `.help-fab` | JIM-mini |
> | `.talk-panel` | QRME |
> | `.waveform` | QRME |
>
> Each time somebody wrote the override, read it back, and had every reason to
> believe it worked. The browser disagreed for a reason that is one sentence long
> and easy to forget: **a media query adds no specificity.**
> `@media (max-height: 600px) { .thing { … } }` and `.thing { … }` are both
> `(0, 1, 0)`, so between them the later source order wins — even though one is a
> narrow, conditional, obviously more specific-*sounding* thing than the other.
>
> ```
> asked     is there a rule that overrides it
> mattered  is that the rule the browser uses
> ```
>
> ## What this checks, and what it deliberately does not
>
> For every declaration inside an at-rule block, it looks for a declaration of the
> *same property* on a *textually identical selector* in an unconditioned rule
> *later in the file*. Identical selector text means identical specificity by
> construction, so a hit is never a specificity judgement call — it is always the
> plain source-order rule, and always a dead declaration.
>
> It does not compare different selectors. `.dock .thing` beating `.thing` is the
> cascade working exactly as intended, and a checker that guessed at specificity
> across differing selectors would report those and be ignored within a week.
> A declaration marked `!important` inside the at-rule is skipped, since it beats a
> later plain declaration and is not dead.
>
> ## Three of the four tests are about the checker
>
> The guard passes on this console, and "passes" is what it would also say if the
> checker had quietly stopped working. Those two look identical from outside, so
> the file carries three small sheets and asserts the difference: the checker
> fails on a sample that has the defect, passes the same sample reordered, and
> leaves a more specific later selector alone.
>
> Two of this estate's proof tests once passed vacuously. A checker whose only
> evidence is a green run is not evidence.
>
> ## Scope here
>
> This console is already clean — the `.vault-light` instance was fixed in an
> earlier round. Nothing in `app/src/styles.css` changes. The guard is added so it
> stays clean, and registered in `shared_guards.txt` (546 → 550) alongside its
> copies in the two sibling products.
>
> ## Testing
>
> Full suite green over the final tree: **1560 passed, 4 skipped, 0 failed.**
>

## #200 — Informal register, two key refusals translated, and two guards that were measuring the wrong thing

- merged · opened 2026-08-24 · merged 2026-08-24
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/200>

> Four pieces, all in `[Unreleased]`.
>
> ## 1. German finishes the informal register
>
> **33 rows** across `app/src/l10n.ts` and the three native `L10n` tables, converted fragment by fragment.
>
> ```
> asked     is the string translated
> mattered  who does it think it is talking to
> ```
>
> Two rows stay counted and neither is convertible, both named in `pdi/tests/formal_register.txt`: `co.bequests.note` (*Sie zu erstellen gewährt nichts* — *to create **it*** grants nothing) and `ntr.intake.sub` (*Sie authentifiziert sich* — the counterparty, not the reader).
>
> ## 2. Two refusals about the tenant's own key
>
> Working the refusal backlog, two of eleven turned out to be reader-facing: `provider must be 'held' or 'kms'` (`Custody.tsx` calls `PUT /key`) and `PDI_MASTER_KEY must be base64 of 32 bytes` — an operator's misconfiguration, but the sentence answers whoever asked for the seal, and the person who reads it is not the person who can fix it. Both hand-translated into all nine languages. Backlog 11 → 9.
>
> Recorded but not acted on: the eight remaining rows are raised in `pdi/problems.py`, whose only client is `sendProblems` in `app/src/errors.ts` — it reads `res.ok` and returns the literal `"failed"`, discarding the body. No reader meets those sentences in any language. That is a decision about the record rather than a translation, and QRME already made the identical call once for its admin-token sentence without generalising it.
>
> ## 3. A wiring precondition is not a refusal
>
> The refusal collector derives which exception classes to follow from the handlers that stringify them — correctly picking up `ValueError` — then swept **every** raise of that class, constructors included.
>
> ```
> asked     is this sentence raised through a class a route stringifies
> mattered  can a person reach the place it is raised
> ```
>
> `PDIClient needs base_url or an injected client` fires in an `__init__` from environment variables while the app is wired: no object, no request, no reader. Two rows here, seven across the trio. Checked three ways — forward, backward, and that the exempted set matches what the ledger names exactly.
>
> ## 4. A floor at a tenth of what it measures
>
> `assert len(i18n._REFUSALS) >= 8` against a table of **84**. Registered as a Ratchet so the comparison happens rather than being assumed, and struck from the unregistered-floor backlog (52 → 51).
>
> ## Verification
>
> Full suite green over this tree: **1331 passed, 4 skipped**. Placeholder parity checked in both directions. Both new guards sabotage-tested.

## #196 — A step that got no model is not a finished step

- merged · opened 2026-08-21 · merged 2026-08-21
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/196>

> ## What
>
> The previous round taught `infer()` to answer an honest sentence instead of raising when the configured inference server is down — right for the ask doors, where a person reads the sentence and acts. It quietly broke the step ledger: an `infer.local` step that "succeeded" with the apology sealed it in the vault as a capture, handed it to the next step as its input, and marked the round done. Before that round, the raw socket error at least failed the step.
>
> `_tool_infer` now checks the answer's provenance: model `local-unreachable` raises `ResidentError` with the same sentence, the runner marks the step failed with it, the task lands `failed`, and the cycle's ledger row carries the reason. The ask doors keep the sentence, exactly as designed.
>
>     asked     did the model answer this step
>     mattered  a ledger that absorbs an apology is a forged ledger
>
> ## Tests
>
> - `test_a_step_that_got_no_model_is_a_failed_step` added to `pdi/tests/test_the_posture_proves_the_model_answers.py`: plan an `infer.local` task against a dead daemon → run → task `failed`, step `failed`, error wears the "did not answer" sentence.
> - Full suite: **1283 passed, 4 skipped**.

## #195 — The posture proves the model answers

- merged · opened 2026-08-21 · merged 2026-08-21
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/195>

> ## What
>
> `GET /resident` gains `local_model_standing`: a live probe of the configured Ollama daemon's own `/api/tags` (three-second timeout) that says whether the server answers and whether the configured model is actually pulled — with the fix named in the note (`ollama pull …`, or check the container and the network). `None` when no server is configured; never raises, so the status door cannot take its own page down.
>
> `infer()` stops raising raw socket errors mid-conversation: a configured server that does not answer now returns a sentence naming what failed and what still works, under model `local-unreachable`, so a tandem's answered-by line never claims the local model spoke.
>
>     asked     is the local model there
>     mattered  proven by a round trip, or read off the environment
>
> ## Why
>
> The posture reported `local_model` alone — the promise, the name an operator wrote into `PDI_RESIDENT_MODEL`. The deploy runbook's §8 produces exactly two failures that name cannot show: the daemon down or on the wrong Docker network, or the daemon up with the model never pulled. Both used to surface identically — a raw urllib traceback out of the ask door.
>
> ## Tests
>
> - `pdi/tests/test_the_posture_proves_the_model_answers.py` — 8 new tests: no-url → `None`; pulled model (including `:latest` tag matching) stands; missing model names the pull; dead daemon is named, not raised; the posture route carries the standing and survives a dead daemon; `infer` answers a sentence.
> - Full suite: **1282 passed, 4 skipped**.

## #193 — The reading tools refuse a recording

- merged · opened 2026-08-20 · merged 2026-08-20
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/193>

> PDI's slice of a multi-repo batch round — its first real change since 0.94.0.
>
> `fetch.listen` was built because a plain fetch of an .mp4 seals compressed video where a person hears a sentence. The planner learned to route media fragments to the listening tool, but the direct doors never did: `fetch.url` or `fetch.render` called straight at a recording still stripped markup from binary and sealed the mojibake as a capture.
>
> Both readers now refuse a media URL by name of the door that hears it (`fetch.listen`), on the same canonical suffix list the qrme briefcase and lookout read — ported into `pdi/ears.py` as `looks_like_recording`, path-only with query stripped, so a page that merely contains a player is still a page and the two stacks call the same bytes a recording.
>
> ```
> asked     can the readers tell a page from a recording
> mattered  a capture of noise wears the same seal as a capture of words
> ```
>
> ## Verification
>
> Full PDI suite over the final tree: **1274 passed, 4 skipped** in 2:07 — including the new `test_the_reading_tools_refuse_a_recording.py` (suffix-list pins, both doors refuse naming the listener, the listener untouched).

## #189 — The capture grows ears — fetch.listen

- merged · opened 2026-08-20 · merged 2026-08-20
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/189>

> asked     what was said in this recording
>     mattered  the words, made at home — never the bytes shipped out
>
> `fetch.render` gave the resident eyes; a recording still answered every fetch with bytes — compressed video sealed where a person hears a sentence. `fetch.listen` joins the vocabulary: the deployment's transcription sidecar (`docker/ears` in the deploy repo, named by `PDI_EARS_URL`) downloads the recording, runs a local speech-to-text model on the facility's own hardware, and what gets sealed is the words — the same capture shape and change-memory the fetches keep, so a standing listen notices when a recording's words change and a re-posted file with the same sentences is not news.
>
> Unlike the eyes there is no honest stand-in (the shell of a page is still the page's text; the bytes of a recording are not its words): a deployment without ears refuses in words — the runs ledger carries the reason — rather than sealing silence or bytes as a transcript.
>
> Changes:
> - `pdi/ears.py` (new): `PDI_EARS_URL` client, `EarsUnavailable`, offline gate on the target
> - `pdi/resident.py`: `_tool_fetch_listen` sealing via the shared `_seal_capture` (with `transcribed`, `duration_seconds`, `language`); registry entry (`leaves_host: true`); planner hears "listen"/"transcribe"/"hear", checked before the fetch verbs
> - `pdi/tests/test_the_capture_grows_ears.py`: six tests — words sealed with duration and language, no-ears refusal in words, dead-ears reason, standing-listen change memory, planner routing, registry honesty
> - `pdi/tests/test_the_database_is_smart.py`: the leaves-host pin now names the three fetches
>
> Full PDI suite green over this tree: 1270 passed, 4 skipped. The sidecar itself ships in the qrme deploy repo alongside the renderer.
>

## #188 — The runs ledger cannot edit its own account

- merged · opened 2026-08-20 · merged 2026-08-20
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/188>

> asked     can the ledger's past be quietly rewritten
>     mattered  a ledger that can edit its own account is a diary in pencil
>
> Raised by the outside reviewer: the runs ledger — the table behind "what did the vault do while you slept", the one the lookouts' trouble lines read — was mutable. Three locks, layered:
>
> - **The database itself refuses edits.** A trigger aborts any UPDATE on `resident_runs` with the rule in its own words, whoever holds the connection — not a code-review promise, a constraint. Deletes stay legal: the trim window, a cancel, and a tenant's erasure are rights.
> - **Each row chains to the task's previous cycle** the way the audit table chains the deployment. `GET /resident/tasks/{id}/runs/verify` walks the links oldest-first — a forged replacement or a deleted middle breaks them. The trim window does not read as tampering, and rows minted before the chain report as `predate_chain` rather than being retroactively invented.
> - **Every cycle anchors its hash on the permanent audit chain** via the `resident.task` entry's ref. The ledger answers "lately"; the audit answers "ever" — even a deleted ledger row leaves its shadow where nothing edits.
>
> The console's Resident screen carries the door (a verify button beside Past rounds, verdict in ten languages); the three native shells record the route in their backlogs with the reason. Existing databases migrate additively. Suite green over the final tree: 1264 passed, 4 skipped — seven new tests covering the refused edit, the intact walk, the forged row, the deleted middle, the trim window, the audit anchor, and the pre-chain rows.
>

## #187 — The capture grows eyes

- merged · opened 2026-08-20 · merged 2026-08-20
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/187>

> asked     what does the page say
>     mattered  what a person would see, not what the server sent first
>
> A JavaScript application answers a plain fetch with an empty shell and a title — a dozen characters standing where a whole console is. The owner demonstrated it live: briefcase items reading "read once — 12 characters" against pages that hold whole products.
>
> `fetch.render` joins the resident's vocabulary: the page rendered in the deployment's browser, sealed as what a person would see. The eyes are a sidecar the deploy stack ships (`pdi/renderer.py` asks it, named by `PDI_RENDERER_URL`) — the vault's own image stays lean. A deployment without eyes, or whose eyes fail, falls back to the plain fetch and **the seal itself says so** (`rendered: false`, `render_fallback` with the reason): an honest shell beats a silent one. The rendered capture keeps the plain fetch's memory — the seal logic is shared, so the fingerprint still says when the page actually changed. The planner hears "render" and "see" as the eyes; the published registry carries the fallback behavior in its own description; the posture guard that pins exactly which tools leave the host learned the new truth consciously.
>
> Suite green over the final tree: 1257 passed, 4 skipped. Seven new tests: rendered seal, honest shell fallback (unset and dead sidecar), change-memory across rendered recaptures, registry honesty, planner keywords, and the offline-gate path.

## #184 — The vault remembers its rounds

- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/184>

> A standing task's step rows reset each cycle — that keeps the plan honest and erases the history: after a week of hourly fetches, the task window showed the latest cycle and nothing else.
>
>     asked     what did the vault do while you slept
>     mattered  a heartbeat nobody can audit is a rumor
>
> Every cycle now lands one row on a per-task runs ledger (`resident_runs`): when it ran, `done` or `failed`, and one line of note — the failing step's error or the last step's summary, capped, never a copy of anything sealed.
>
> - `GET /resident/tasks/{tid}/runs` reads it newest first, behind the same tenant fence as every task door: another tenant's ledger is a task that does not exist.
> - The ledger answers "lately", not "ever" — the last 200 rounds are kept, and the audit chain stays the permanent record.
> - A cancel takes the ledger with the task, the same way it takes the steps; a tenant wipe sweeps `resident_runs` like every tenant-scoped table, because the cascade derives its list from the schema.
> - A "Past rounds" button on each task, on the console and all three shells, failed rounds worn in red.
>

## #183 — The fetch notices change

- merged · opened 2026-08-19 · merged 2026-08-19
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/183>

> A standing fetch overwrites the same seal each cycle — that kept the capture current and lost the one thing a watcher wants to know: did the page change, or was it merely fetched again?
>
>     asked     did the page change, or was it merely fetched again
>     mattered  a watcher who cannot tell is re-reading, not watching
>
> The capture now carries a fingerprint and its change history:
>
> - **`sha`** — sha256 of the fetched text, shortened: enough to compare, not an archive of anything.
> - **`changed_at`** — kept when the content is identical to the prior seal, moved to the fetch time when it differs, so the seal remembers when the page last *actually* changed across every overwrite.
> - **`first_seen_at`** — set at the first capture, carried through every change since.
>
> A seal from before fingerprints gets one derived from its own text, so an identical page is never reported changed just because the seal gained a field — and its stand-in dates are its own fetch time, never now, which would invent a fresh change out of a schema migration. The step summary says which case each cycle was — `(first capture)`, `(unchanged)`, `(changed)` — readable in the task window the clients already have. No new routes; nothing quoted.
>
> The tandems' lookouts (davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini#231, davidsbianchi1984/qrme#303) inherit this for free the next time their vaults re-seal: the capture JSON they already read simply knows more.
>
> Full suite green over this tree: 1245 passed, 4 skipped.

> `DELETE /resident/tasks/{tid}` ends a task's future — a standing task stops keeping its appointment — while the audit chain and whatever the runs already wrote (dataset rows, sealed fetches) stay, because a cancel ends the future, not the record.

## #177 — Standing tasks: the vault keeps its own appointments

- merged · opened 2026-08-19 · merged 2026-08-19
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/177>

> A plan can carry `every_hours` (a quarter-hour to a month), and the resident re-runs it on that interval itself. The "no separate orchestration service" claim extended to *when*, not just *what*: a fetch-and-table errand that refreshes itself weekly needs no cron, no worker, no caller.
>
>     asked     can the vault run its own errands
>     mattered  who has to remember the appointment
>
> - `pulse()` walks the tenants and then each tenant's due tasks, so **every statement on a tenant-scoped table stays constrained to one tenant** — the isolation fence holds on the heartbeat exactly as it does on the request paths. `PDI_RESIDENT_PULSE` (seconds) starts the in-process loop; unset (tests, ad-hoc runs), nothing ticks and `pulse` stays a function a caller can drive.
> - `done` is a resting state for a standing task, not a terminal one: its steps reset and the same plan executes whole each cycle. The next appointment is kept whatever the cycle did — a failing task retries rather than going silent — a run already in flight is never doubled by an overlapping beat, and a deleted tenant's appointments never fire.
> - Every pulse-run lands on the audit chain as `resident.pulse`; the posture reports the standing-task count and the heartbeat interval.
> - The plan form on the console and all three shells gains the "repeats every (hours)" field (also added to the field-label vocabulary in all ten languages), the next appointment shows on the task line, and the Run button stays on a standing task even when done — it is meant to run again, by hand as much as by the beat.
> - `resident_tasks` grows the two columns through the additive-migration path, so a vault created before them keeps working.
>
> Eight new tests: the appointment is stored and rescheduled, out-of-window intervals refuse, the pulse runs what is due and only what is due, a done standing task runs whole again, deleted tenants never fire, manual re-runs are allowed while one-shots keep their 409, and the posture counts. Full suite: **1229 passed, 4 skipped**.

## #172 — Tenant isolation in the SQL, the resident intelligence, and the 0.86.0 cut

- merged · opened 2026-08-18 · merged 2026-08-18
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/172>

> Three commits:
>
> **A tenant's row is reached through its tenant, in the SQL itself.** Forty-six statements on tenant-scoped tables were keyed by bare `id`, trusting a Python check a few lines up. Every one now constrains `tenant_id` in the statement — reads, updates, deletes and counter bumps across connectors, apps, robots, transfers, intakes, bequests, beacons, rings, gate pages, roster, retention, reseals and rewraps. The ten that genuinely cannot be scoped (bearer secrets, public scan surfaces, admin doors, the deployment-wide audit chain) wear inline `# tenant-unscoped:` markers recorded with a ceiling in `tenant_unscoped.txt`. An AST guard reads every `execute()` against the live schema, and live tests drive the fence from outside — "not yours" and "not there" are one answer.
>
> **The database made smart: the resident intelligence.** `pdi/resident.py` — the agent living in the vault process, for tenants in colocation, leased space, their own facility or their own device: multi-step plans by deterministic rules (readable before anything runs, identical with no model), a closed tool registry whose `leaves_host` travels with every step, structured rows into queryable datasets straight from a fetch, embeddings + cosine search storing a hash and never the text, and local-only inference (`PDI_OLLAMA_URL` or the honest stub). Every act on the audit chain under six new catalogued actions; offline mode refuses the one outbound tool at the socket; refusals translated into ten languages. Same doors and privacy over standard HTTPS — doors on the console, iOS, Android and Windows, with a drawn screen (58), a tutorial lesson and assistant phrasings.
>
> **Release prep 0.86.0** — thirteen manifest-driven version fields, CHANGELOG section dated 2026-08-18, README banner and release-table row.
>

## #169 — A guard on the translations that are already there

- merged · opened 2026-08-18 · merged 2026-08-18
- `claude/new-session-ftgm38` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/169>

> `refusals_untranslated.txt` counts the sentences with **no** translation yet and only shrinks. Nothing has ever looked at the ones that do, so the whole backlog can reach zero with a Chinese row written in Cyrillic in it and every guard in this estate reports the paydown as complete.
>
> ```
> asked     is every refusal translated
> mattered  is each translation in the language it is filed under
> ```
>
> ## Where it came from
>
> Two got through that way and were caught by eye rather than by anything: the word `как` inside a Chinese string, and the syllable `각` inside a Japanese one. Both were single characters in otherwise correct sentences, both rendered, and neither failed a test. A reader of that language meets a word from another alphabet at the moment the product is telling them no — the worst moment to look unreliable, and the reason the refusals were translated first.
>
> ## What it asks
>
> Per language, the script that language is written in — plus the two failures the backlog file structurally cannot see:
>
> - a value **identical to the English key**, which is a row added to make the count go up
> - a row that does not carry **every** language, which the backlog cannot see because a row is either in it or not
>
> Deliberately narrow. It cannot tell a good translation from a poor one and does not pretend to; it catches *this is not that language at all*, which is the class that got through twice. Both original defects were re-introduced to confirm it catches them.
>
> Byte-identical in QRME, JIM-mini and PDI (`3e205f0…`), like `release_fields.txt` — the defect is the estate's and so is the check.
>
> ## What it found
>
> Nothing, which is the answer to an open question. All three tables are clean: QRME 296 rows, JIM-mini 135, PDI **64**, every row carrying all nine languages, none a pass-through of the English, none in the wrong script. The handoff recorded `refusals_untranslated.txt` at 216 rows; it is at 109, so the paydown continued after that note was written and nothing was lost.
>
> ## Testing
>
> - Full local suite over the final tree: **1177 passed, 4 skipped** (2:17) — +18 against 0.84.0's 1159, which is this file.
>

## #168 — Release prep 0.84.0

>

> asked     which files does a release touch
> mattered  which fields does a release touch

>
> `0.82.0 -> 0.84.0`, build code `82000 -> 84000`.
>
> ## No code changes in this product, and the number crosses two
>
> 0.83.0 was JIM-mini's address-book round and QRME's deploy-page repair. Neither touched anything here, so the number was left where it was rather than cut for the sake of it — and that is exactly the drift the version guard exists to prevent. A box carrying 0.83.0 on two ports and 0.82.0 on the third reports the mismatch to whoever is using it rather than to whoever deployed it.
>
> So this release says plainly that nothing changed in this product, in the shape 0.80.0 already used for the same situation, and brings the three back onto one number. Cheaper than a scheme where they drift apart and somebody has to remember which pairs go together.

## #167 — The error reports come home, then a field report puts a light on the menu

- merged · opened 2026-08-13 · merged 2026-08-13
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/167>

> Twenty-one commits: the error collector, seven bites of the shells&#39; doorless backlog run all the way (**86 → 0 rows per shell**), the tabs-untranslated ratchet run to zero, three bites of the three-repo guard-divergences backlog, and a field report from the beta that turned out to be four defects wearing one coat.
>
> ## The collector (`29ee546`)
>
> `POST /v1/problems` on this backend (whitelist contract self-contained in `pdi/problems.py`; 422 names the field; rows fold into counters). `GET /v1/problems` behind `PDI_PROBLEMS_KEY` or the backend&#39;s own machine, refusals ×9. Console fallback + retrieval half ×10; the Windows shell reads the aggregate. New table only: `problem_reports`.
>
> ## Guard-estate repairs (`350563c` + `531e3df`) and the guide (`c4bd4eb`)
>
> The member-guard&#39;s Kotlin reader learned nested classes are members (`shared_guards.txt` 468 → 469 byte-identical ×3); floor records re-pointed; the problems lesson teaches the retrieval half.
>
> ## Seven doorless bites — 86 doors per shell, backlog to zero
>
> **The sticker family (`e4981c5` + `aff9a29`, 86 → 70):** a Carriers segment on the Transfers screen — custody codes placed and walked along their chain, the stranger&#39;s card, found/ring with no bearer, landing + QR as URL-builder doors, Who rang with transcripts, the pairing card, the recipient-link check.
>
> **Tenants + the agent at the gate (`dea92f7`, 70 → 54):** the operator&#39;s whole tenant surface behind `PDI_ADMIN_TOKEN`, and the gate&#39;s ceiling/channel/roster/timezone/pages-with-retry.
>
> **What outlives the tenant (`24c3d31`, 54 → 39):** bequests through their whole life including the heir&#39;s two-secrets redeem; contributions; snapshot/restore; retention window, sweep, seed.
>
> **The posture family (`6677265`, 39 → 30):** health, the hosting arrangement with both sides&#39; responsibilities and one-press moves, operations, the audit vocabulary, the BAA standing. The wire guards caught BAA names promised on a route that never sends them and a `version` about to mean two types — both fixed their way.
>
> **Exchange details + the builder (`800f003`, 30 → 22):** every transfer row re-readable with its custody chain&#39;s verdict and receivable as the recipient — the one-shot receive token riding as its own header, because the recipient is not the tenant; every intake row the same; and Positions, the assistant builder from the smallest honest intake.
>
> **Whose hands the key is in (`3125a79`, 22 → 13):** BYOK custody on all three phones — adopt a held key, move to KMS, hand custody back, reseal everything under a fresh version, with the wire&#39;s own sentence about who can decrypt rendered as the answer; the minted-token revoke beside the mint; the connected-apps catalog count and each publish connector&#39;s own beacon + QR; the robot unbind.
>
> **The guide, the pane and the translator (`01c66de`, 13 → 0):** the console&#39;s walkthrough stepped through from each phone, a question box for the assistant, one of PDI&#39;s own notes translated with the engine field shown, and the pane in the corner with its public vocabulary and this tenant&#39;s arrangement.
>
> ## The last English behind the tabs (`1696d91`, 6/5/1 → 0/0/0)
>
> The tabs-untranslated ratchet run to zero on all three shells: the key-management card&#39;s provider label rides the console&#39;s own `cu.provider` row; interpolation fragments hoisted to locals so the scanner reads words people see, not regex shrapnel; the Welcome screen&#39;s default-address literal restructured. All shell l10n copied byte-identical from the console&#39;s key sets; floors raised to four-fifths of the estate the doors built.
>
> ## The light that sat on the menu (`cf4137a`, `0f179bc`, `041c229`)
>
> From the beta, on a phone: **&#34;It seems to be blocking the PDI menus&#34;** — the vault light, the pill reading *vault answering · v0.68.0*, sitting on top of the bottom tab bar with Overview, Tenants and half of Bridges underneath it. Then a second photograph one state along: **&#34;Same thing with the small green circle when minimized&#34;**.
>
> **1. The light sat on the menu.** Both halves were correct alone: the light is `position: fixed; bottom: 22px`, page margin on a desktop; the sidebar becomes a bottom bar under `@media (max-width: 760px)`, the ordinary way to put navigation on a phone. Neither rule knew about the other, and no test in this suite had ever read the stylesheet — every guard here asks what a screen *says*, and this was a question about where a thing *sits*.
>
>     asked     is every screen wired, translated and reachable
>     mattered  can the person's thumb reach the tab under the light
>
> `test_the_light_sat_on_the_menu.py` measures the bar out of the stylesheet&#39;s own numbers — `.nav-item` min-height plus `.sidebar` padding — so the clearance tracks the bar.
>
> **2. Minimized was not small.** The dot was 40px, solid, heavy-shadowed — the footprint of a button with none of the explanation. Minimizing is the reader saying *get out of the way*, and a widget that answers by dropping its words while keeping its size has not obeyed. On a phone it is now 22px at 0.75 opacity — QRME&#39;s numbers, arrived at after the same report about the same widget — with both as named ceilings in the guard, proven failing on the reported CSS.
>
> **3. The light spoke English on a console that translates everything else.** Eleven strings across six screens, hard-coded beside code that imports the translator. Eleven rows enter `l10n.ts` in ten languages, and every site selects the *result* rather than the key — `ok ? t("vl.answering", lang) : t("vl.degraded", lang)` — because the dead-key guard reads literals after `t(`.
>
> **4. The reader could not see any of it.** `{ok ? "vault answering" : "vault degraded"}` is a `ConditionalExpression`, not a `JsxText` node, so a sentence chosen at render time was text to whoever read the screen and nothing at all to whoever read the source.
>
>     asked     what text does this file place between its tags
>     mattered  what words does this screen put on the glass
>
> `scripts/jsx-text.mjs` now reads string literals in child position — both branches of a ternary, either side of `&&`/`||`/`??`, the pieces of a concatenation — skipping literals with no letter in them, and still refusing call arguments. `0f179bc` is what that widening found in this suite&#39;s own guards: the audit-vocabulary check was the last one greping a screen for its English, and it now follows `_says` like its neighbours.
>
> ## Three divergence bites (`5fb2a44`, `abc048d`, `b4c5ecd`, `c7740cf`) — 136 → 121
>
> **The estate records follow the JIM port (`5fb2a44`, 136 → 124):** twelve rows resolved by porting the questions rather than the names.
>
> **The vault asks whether holding is owning (`abc048d`, 124 → 122).** Both rows found something on arrival:
>
> - `test_every_test_count_the_readme_claims_is_true` found `pdi/hosting.py` claiming 16 tests while its two test files hold 25. One claim here names two modules at once, so the guard counts every module a claim names as one bucket, and `test_console_guide.py` became `test_tutorial.py` — a test file the convention cannot find is a claim nothing checks.
> - `test_custody_is_never_described_as_ownership` was recorded as missing from **PDI**, which is the wrong way round: this is the product whose whole job is holding data about people who are not its customer. Nothing a tenant is shown said that PDI&#39;s holding confers nothing, or that the rights of the people the records are *about* survive it. Both sentences now sit in the words a tenant reads — a key point on `GET /terms`, clause 2.1a in `docs/terms.md`, a line on the hosting guidance — and the terms version moves **1.2 → 1.3**, with a second guard binding the served version to the document&#39;s stated one.
>
> **The light guard reads two stylesheets more (`b4c5ecd`, shared 483 → 487):** QRME and JIM-mini already behaved correctly but neither had anything asking the question. The port also repaired the reader: the siblings open the mobile media query more than once so their corner widgets win the cascade from the bottom of the file.
>
> **The apology for a failed route (`c7740cf`, 122 → 121, shared 487 → 489).** Porting `test_every_handler_returns_through_the_one_place` to QRME exposed something none of the three suites was asking. Every exception handler answers in the reader&#39;s language; the catch-all does not, and could not be caught by that guard, because the catch-all is not a handler — `@app.exception_handler(Exception)` sits outside the CORS layer, so it has to be a middleware, and being a middleware nothing was asking it anything. Its sentence sat inline in English in all three products.
>
>     asked     does every exception handler answer in the reader's language
>     mattered  does every failure answer in the reader's language
>
> `i18n.SERVER_ERROR` is a named constant with its row in `_REFUSALS` in nine languages, and the middleware reads the reader&#39;s language the way the handlers do. `test_every_failure_answers_in_the_readers_language` asks the catch-all directly in all three suites, matching a middleware whose `except` names `Exception` (or is bare) — not the JSON-localizing middleware, which catches `(ValueError, TypeError)` around one `json.loads` and is not answering a failed route.
>
> ## Tests
>
> **Full suite green over the final tree: 1130 passed, 3 skipped** (gate over `c7740cf`). All three per-shell doorless records at zero, `native_screens_untranslated.txt` at **0/0/0**, `guard_divergences.txt` at 121 with `shared_guards.txt` at 489.

## #158 — Round 17: the screens behind the tabs speak — PDI's shells drop 65/59/69 to 6/5/1

- merged · opened 2026-08-11 · merged 2026-08-11
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/158>

> ## What this round does
>
> PDI's untranslated record has said since it was written: the vault's tab bar reads *Bóveda*, *Auditoría*, *Transferencias*, and the screens behind them are English. This round closes the whole surface in one pass — the smallest product of the three, and the last one still owing its screens. The record drops from **65/59/69** (iOS/Android/Windows) to **6/5/1**, and every survivor is named.
>
> ### Keyed on all three shells
> - **Overview** — unlocked badge, vault heading, sealed-at-rest sentence, token card, language card with the pre-translate switch, intact/broken stat
> - **Vault** — seal form, empty state, reveal/hide/delete/provenance, and the provenance lines (origin, sealed-bytes, audit-event line with its chain verdict)
> - **Audit** — description, verifying/intact/broken, recorded-events count
> - **Robots** — the sealed promise, bind, the three seal buttons, sealed keys, both read-it-back lines
> - **Connectors** — link sentence, collect/publish, ingest/update, disconnect
> - **Admin** — title, the admin-token warning (split into shared sentence + rotation coda), the four actions, the what-do-you-hold-about-us door
> - **Feedback card** and the **problem-reporting notice**
>
> ### The rows arrived checked
> - Where one shell already held a row the others hardcoded (Android's admin/record keys, Windows' problem-report and so-far rows), the existing row was copied verbatim and asked everywhere rather than minted again.
> - Where the console already says the same English (Disconnect, Platform, Snapshot, Hide, Seal record), the native rows carry the console's translations verbatim — the cross-client guard holds them to it.
> - The one wording that differed between shells (iOS's "tamper-evident audit chain" vs "hash-chained in the audit log") is unified, not translated twice.
> - The dead-key guard caught Android asking for `tab.robots`/`tab.connectors` — rows only its siblings held; copied, not re-minted.
>
> ### Windows moves wording out of XAML
> Attributes are written once at parse time and cannot be re-read when the language changes, so the counted strings move to `x:Name` + code-behind, and buttons inside item templates get labels from row properties.
>
> ### Guards that moved with the surface
> - The consent-drawer guard matched the English answers by wording; it now asks the card for the keys (`prb.send`, `prb.donot`) and the table for the words — matching the key alone would pass with the table saying anything at all.
> - The localizer floors sat at 38–50 against shells that now ask 135–144 rows; the within-sight rule fired as designed, and the floors rise to 120–140.
>
> ## Verification
> Full local suite green before push: **1111 passed, 3 skipped**.

## #149 — The console the policy blanked

- merged · opened 2026-08-10 · merged 2026-08-10
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/149>

> ## What happened
>
> The first real deploy of the beta topology came up healthy — containers, certificates, all of it — and all three consoles rendered as blank dark pages. The nonce Content-Security-Policy meant for the server-rendered pages was stamped on the console's HTML too, and the console's bundle is an external same-origin script no nonce can reach, so the browser refused it. HTML 200, nothing running. No in-process test saw it: a `TestClient` reads the policy and enforces none of it.
>
> ## The fix
>
> - **`pagehead.console_policy()`** — `'self'` where the page policy names a nonce, still no inline script; `blob:` for previews and synthesised audio, `worker-src` for the service worker, `manifest-src` for the home screen.
> - **The middleware picks the policy by path** — `/app` gets the console policy; every other page keeps the nonce policy unchanged.
> - **`GET /` redirects to `/app/`** when a console is built. Measured live, the bare domain answered `{"detail": "Not Found"}`, and a tester types the domain, not the mount point. Recorded in `NOT_A_CLIENT_CALL`: no client constructs the address it is already standing on.
>
> ## Test-enforced
>
> `test_what_the_browser_enforces.py` now measures the console's headers over real HTTP, against a console dist the fixture lays down itself so CI asks the question without a front-end build. Three new guards enter `shared_guards.txt` (450 → 453), byte-identical across the three repositories. Guard on the guard: the stranger pages are asserted to keep their nonce policy, so the console's wider policy cannot leak.
>
> Full suite: 1,094 passed, 5 skipped.
>
> Sibling PRs carry the same fix in qrme and jim-mini.
>
> 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

## #148 — The console round: wired is not finished, and 91 → 32

- merged · opened 2026-08-09 · merged 2026-08-10
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/148>

> Console English **91 → 32**, in two commits: the finding and its guard, then the five screens it cleared the way for.
>
> ## The finding (`d03790f`)
>
> Every localization round picked its next screen off the list in `console_untranslated.txt`, worst count first. That ordering carried an assumption nothing had checked: that a screen already *through* a round is done with.
>
> ```
> asked     does this screen import the translator
> mattered  does this screen still hold English
> ```
>
> Eight files import `t` from `../l10n`. Six held no English. Two had held some since **0.48.3**, the round that claimed them: `Continuity.tsx` (10) and `Custody.tsx` (5). Twelve releases on the finished side of the ledger.
>
> The sharp part is what six of Continuity's ten were — strings the table **already held**:
>
> | String | Key it already had |
> |---|---|
> | `Name` | `co.name.ph` |
> | `Role` | `co.role.ph` |
> | `Read` | `co.read` |
> | `Set` | `co.set` |
> | `What can I open?` | `co.whatopen` |
> | `Nothing is readable under that grant.` | `co.nothing.readable` |
>
> Not a missing translation — one already written, already paid for, and never asked for.
>
> `test_a_screen_that_imports_the_translator_holds_no_english` holds the claim from here: **a screen that asks the table for a word may not also hard-code one.** A file is free to be untranslated; that is what the ceiling is for. What it may not be is half-translated and counted as whole. It failed on the round it was written, naming both files and all fifteen strings.
>
> ## The five screens (`fd8f634`)
>
> Records, Problems, Settings, Tenants, ProblemNotice — 44 counted strings, 76 → 32. Records went first because sealing and opening a record is the thing the product is *for*. Problems and ProblemNotice are one feature in two places: what leaves the device, and the consent asked before anything does.
>
> Seven of the 44 were English the tables already held. Four are the shells' (`nrec.t.records`, `tab.vault`, `nrec.key`, `nrec.seal`) and their wordings are **copied rather than rewritten** — the split `test_the_desktop_and_the_phone_say_the_same_thing` exists to catch, avoided in advance rather than after it went red.
>
> ## The second kind of string
>
> The reader counts JSX text, `placeholder` and `title`. It does not count a string literal inside a JSX *expression* — and a lot of English lives there: `{busy ? "Creating…" : "Create tenant"}`, the whole offline-posture block in Settings, six outcome sentences in a module-level map in Problems. Never counted, always read. Translated here too, which is why the rows added exceed 44. `Problems.tsx`'s `OUTCOME` map now holds keys rather than sentences, since a module-level constant cannot ask for a language.
>
> One shadowing, caught by `tsc` on the first run and not by reading: `const t = await api.createTenant(…)` in Tenants. Second time in three rounds for that shape (0.60.5 had `transfers.map((t) => …)` in Exchange).
>
> ## Still open
>
> Remaining 32: Keys 7, Overview 7, Audit 6, Operations 6, VaultLight 4, App 2.
>
> **No version bump — this is not a cut.** All three products still read 0.60.6.
>
> ## Checks
>
> - PDI 1084 passed, 5 skipped
> - JIM-mini 1741 passed, 3 skipped
> - QRME 3235 passed, 1 skipped
> - `npx tsc --noEmit` clean
> - CI green on both commits (push + pull_request runs). Native apps correctly never triggered — no `native/**` paths touched.

## #147 — PDI console doors: the 84-route backlog reaches zero

- merged · opened 2026-07-31 · merged 2026-07-31
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/147>

> This closes the door audit across all three products. PDI's desktop console could not reach 84 of the vault's routes; every one was present on a phone shell.
>
> Five screens (48–52): **Carriers** (a sealed thing and the code on the outside of it), **Exchange** (what leaves sealed and what is asked to come in), **Custody** (the key, the hardware, the paperwork), **Bridges** (what reaches into the vault), **Guiding** (the console's own guide). Each with a walkthrough lesson, assistant phrasing, a drawing and a manifest row.
>
> All three record files are now **empty rather than short**, and the tests that read them assert emptiness.
>
> ## What driving the routes found
>
> Nothing in the vault was broken. Six places where the route table and the wire disagree:
>
> - **`receive` and `submit` take tokens of their own, in headers of their own** — `x-receive-token` and `x-submit-token`, not the tenant's bearer token. The party receiving a transfer is a clinic and the party submitting to an intake is a records office; neither is the tenant, has a tenant credential, or should. Bound as bearer credentials both are a 403 every time.
> - **The scan page is HTML and two `qr.svg` routes are SVG.** PDI's `req` runs `JSON.parse` unguarded, so binding them through it did not return the wrong thing — it threw `SyntaxError: Unexpected token <` from inside the client, which names nothing.
> - **A key provider is `held` or `kms`** — not `customer`, which is what the concept is called in the plan copy, in the hosting guarantees, and in the field `customer_managed` two lines from the one that rejects it.
> - **A beacon's `disclose` is a single value**, `blind` or `contact`, not the list of fields to reveal the name suggests.
> - **`ref_kind` is one of four** and a ring's `kind` one of four others.
> - **A token's role is `read` or `write`.**
>
> Three of those the server answers with the exact set of legal values in its 422 body, so the unions in `api.ts` are transcribed from the vault rather than invented.
>
> Adding the text helper then made three of this round's own doors invisible to `clientpaths` — the third extractor false positive after the nested template and the `<img src>`.
>
> ## Two guards that could only pass while the problem existed
>
> Same pair as in JIM, broken the same way: one asserted the union backlog was *strictly* smaller than the console's, the other asserted the snapshot file was non-empty. A check that can only be satisfied by the problem still existing is not a check.
>
> ## The three orphaned bindings
>
> - The **audit action glossary** now sits on the screen that lists the actions. A log whose vocabulary is undocumented where it is read is a log somebody has to guess at during an incident.
> - The gateway says **whether a channel is configured**, so an empty page list is no longer read as a quiet week when it means nothing could have been sent.
> - **Revoking a grant token** is offered as its own act, distinct from revoking the bequest that minted it — only the softer one had a button.
>
> ## Verification
>
> Full suite: **359 passed, 1 skipped**. Console typecheck clean. Each fix injection-tested.
>

## #145 — The release title carries the product name

- merged · opened 2026-07-31 · merged 2026-07-31
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/145>

> Docs only. Records two things 0.20.1's tagging taught, in `docs/releasing.md` (all three repositories).
>
> ## The title carries the product name
>
> `PDI app-v0.20.1`, not `app-v0.20.1`. Three repositories cut the same number in the same pass, so a release list, a notification and a search result otherwise show three entries reading `app-v0.20.1` with nothing to tell them apart. The worked example link in the doc now reads `&title=PDI%20app-v0.19.0`.
>
> This repository's releases before 0.20.1 carry no name at all, so it is the one where the difference is most visible.
>
> ## A draft creates no tag
>
> Saving the release form as a draft stores the release and leaves the tag unmade — the draft's URL says `untagged-…`. So the release workflow never fires, no installers are built, and `sync-release-notes.yml` never lays `RELEASE_NOTES.md` over the body, because the thing all of it listens for is the tag push. That is also why *Generate release notes* has nothing to offer on a draft: it needs a tag to diff against.
>
> Worth writing down because the failure is silent in the way this repository keeps finding: the release exists, it looks finished, and none of the machinery behind it has run.
>

## #143 — Ask each client the door question separately

- merged · opened 2026-07-31 · merged 2026-07-31
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/143>

> `clientpaths.doorless` unions the console with the iOS, Android and Windows shells, so a route only the phone calls counts as doored. PDI's union backlog reads **58**; the console alone cannot reach **84 routes**. The guard was answering *some client can reach this*, which was true, in place of *this client can reach this*, which was not.
>
> ## Added
>
> - **`test_the_console_is_a_client_too.py`** — the console's own backlog in `console_doorless.txt`, checked in both directions and ratcheted so it cannot grow past where it started. The union guard stays; a route no client anywhere calls is still worse. A phone-only capability is a legitimate design choice, which is what the snapshot is for: deferring one takes a deliberate edit and shows up in a diff.
> - **`test_a_binding_is_not_a_door.py`** — the same mistake one level down. A function in `api.ts` that no screen calls is not a door, and `doorless` counts it as one. The docstring on `doorless` called this "a discipline rather than something the test can enforce"; it turned out to be enforceable in about twenty lines. *The test cannot check this* is a claim worth testing.
>
> ## Fixed
>
> - **`clientpaths.py` was not byte-identical across the three repositories**, though it says it is. This copy never received the `fetch`, `window.open`, `<img src>` and `<a href>` call forms from the previous round.
> - **The pairing QR is built from a literal.** `Settings.tsx` rendered it as `getBase() + pair.qr_svg`, where the path arrives in a response body — a real door no static check can see. `GET /pair/qr.svg` sat in `NOT_A_CLIENT_CALL` for exactly that reason, which is an exemption made out of a blind spot; the last one of those turned out to have no door at all. Same request, now visible to the audit.
>
> ## Verification
>
> Full suite: **340 passed, 1 skipped**. Console typechecks.

## #142 — The installer could not report, and nothing said so

- merged · opened 2026-07-31 · merged 2026-07-31
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/142>

> Found in QRME while going to write the setup instructions for `PROBLEM_COLLECTOR`, then checked here and in JIM-mini. **All three had it.**
>
> ## The chain, and where it broke
>
> | link | state |
> |---|---|
> | `errors.ts` reads `__PROBLEM_COLLECTOR__` | ✅ |
> | `vite.config.ts` defines it from `process.env.PROBLEM_COLLECTOR` | ✅ |
> | `docs/cloud-model.md` says to rebuild with it set | ✅ |
> | **`desktop-release.yml` passes it to the build** | ❌ |
>
> Three packaging steps — macOS signed, Windows signed, unsigned — each carefully threading its *signing certificate* through `env:`, and not one of them mentioning the collector.
>
> So **every installer this workflow has ever produced compiled an empty address**, whatever secrets were configured, and launch-time error reporting was inert in every shipped desktop build. Nothing failed. The define existed. The documentation was correct about what to set. The step that runs the build dropped it on the floor.
>
> `errors.ts` already anticipates the *neighbouring* mistake — it refuses to invent a product name because *"a repo whose vite.config.ts forgot the define would otherwise file its reports under whichever product this fallback named"*. The define was not forgotten. The environment feeding it was.
>
> ## The guard
>
> `test_the_installer_can_actually_report.py` checks the **chain**, not any one link. Build steps are found by what they run (`npm run dist`), not by what they are called — the name is the part most likely to change — and a guard-on-guard fails if that finder ever returns fewer than three, so a renamed command cannot make the file quietly vacuous.
>
> `every` rather than `some` matters: the workflow branches three ways on signing and a build comes from exactly one branch per platform, so a variable threaded through two of three still ships one silent platform.
>
> **Four injections verified**, including unwiring one step of three — the defect that shipped.
>
> ## Behaviour that did not change
>
> An unset secret still builds: it arrives as an empty string, exactly the state the source already reads as *no collector*. "Off by default" survives the wiring.
>
> ## What this changes on your side
>
> Set `PROBLEM_COLLECTOR` and `PROBLEM_TOKEN` as repository **secrets**, then re-run the desktop release. Setting them before this fix would have done nothing.
>
> `docs/cloud-model.md` stays byte-identical across the three repositories, verified by md5.
>
> **Suite: 332 passed, 1 skipped.**
>

## #141 — Cut 0.20.0

- merged · opened 2026-07-31 · merged 2026-07-31
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/141>

> All three repositories move together, as they always do — one number across QRME, JIM-mini and PDI, so a console and a backend that disagree about their version are disagreeing about something real.
>
> Five version strings: `pyproject.toml`, the `FastAPI(version=)` in `pdi/api.py`, `app/package.json`, and both root entries of `app/package-lock.json`.
>
> ## Two rounds since 0.19.1
>
> ### The native shells record what breaks
>
> The consoles have logged failures **content-free** since 0.19.0 — the operation and the status, never the message, never the path as it was typed. That is the governing constraint: a crash report is worth having only if nothing private travels in it, and the safest way to guarantee that is to have nothing private to send.
>
> The web console has done it since 0.19.0. **iOS, Android and the desktop shell had not** — so a failure that happened only on a phone happened only in silence. All three now record on the same terms and post to the same gateway.
>
> `docs/cloud-model.md` (byte-identical across the three repositories) gains the gateway's container deploy path. The gateway lives in QRME's tree, but every product's console posts to it, so the instructions belong wherever somebody is reading about the contract.
>
> ### A guard that invented work
>
> Every earlier defect in `clientpaths.py` made it too **lenient**: a truncated path, a verb read off a neighbouring call, a route table read flat instead of recursed. Those are the failures you expect from a checker, and the ones its guard-on-guard was written to catch.
>
> This one was the other kind. A template literal may nest another inside an interpolation, and the extraction pattern's backtick alternative stopped at the *inner* opening backtick — so a call normalised to a path no route matches, and a route with a working door was reported as having none.
>
> Nothing failed. The suite stayed green. The route sat on the backlog looking like work, and a door-building round was aimed at a door that already existed. **A checker that invents work fails more quietly than one that misses some:** a miss is found by the bug it let through, while an invention is found only by somebody going to do the work and finding it done. Interpolations are now matched by counting braces, so a nested one passes through intact.
>
> ---
>
> `CHANGELOG.md` and `RELEASE_NOTES.md` updated. **Suite: 324 passed, 1 skipped.** Console typecheck clean.
>
> The tag is not created here — that stays yours.
>

## #140 — Record what breaks on the phone and the desktop shell too

- merged · opened 2026-07-30 · merged 2026-07-30
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/140>

> The console has recorded failures content-free since 0.19.0 — the operation and the status, never the message, never the path as it was actually called. The three native shells recorded nothing at all, so every failure a user hit on iOS, Android or Windows was invisible.
>
> ## The rule, in three more languages
>
> | | |
> |---|---|
> | `native/ios/Sources/Problems.swift` | `UserDefaults`, `Codable` rows |
> | `native/android/…/Problems.kt` | `SharedPreferences`, JSON rows |
> | `native/windows/Problems.cs` | `%LOCALAPPDATA%/pdi`, `System.Text.Json` |
>
> Each takes `method`, `path`, `status` and has no parameter a detail string could arrive through. The signature is the safeguard, and it matters here specifically: the backends put user input straight into their error messages. Good messages for the person reading them and the wrong thing to keep, so they are shown and never written down. Redaction happens on the way *in*, so the stored buffer never holds a value that would later have to be scrubbed.
>
> `GET /records/{id} → 500` identifies a bug; `GET /records/rec_0de08e794ed0` identifies a person. Only the first survives.
>
> ## Why the tests are structural
>
> One rule with four implementations drifts, and it drifts silently — a redaction narrowed on Android leaks nothing on the desktop, so nothing an ordinary test run would notice. There is no test runner for these sources here; the native workflow compiles them and stops. So `test_native_shells_record_nothing_private.py` reads them the way the TypeScript guard reads `errors.ts`: signature arity, stored fields, the four redaction patterns at full width, the FNV-1a constants, and both failure kinds at the call sites — including the request that never reached a server, which is the case an implementation forgets because it is an exception rather than a status.
>
> What it cannot check is behaviour: that Swift's FNV-1a and Kotlin's produce the same digits is asserted by neither, only that both are FNV-1a with the same constants. Stated in the file rather than hidden.
>
> ## The defect writing that guard exposed
>
> `Problems.attach` existed and was called nowhere. The Android shell would have recorded nothing and said nothing about it, because the recorder refuses to crash over a diagnostic — a missing attach has no symptom at all. Every structural check above passed while the feature was simply off on that platform.
>
> Worth naming as a class rather than a typo: those checks ask whether each piece is *correct*, and correctness of every piece is not the same as the feature being *on*. iOS and Windows have no equivalent step, which is exactly why the third platform's extra wire went unnoticed. `MainActivity.onCreate` now attaches, and `test_the_android_recorder_is_switched_on` fails if that line ever leaves.
>
> ## Scope
>
> These record only. Sending stays the console's job and happens only where a collector was compiled in, so native-shell failures do not reach the gateway aggregate. `native/README.md` says so rather than leaving it to be assumed.
>
> Also carries the previously-pushed gateway container deploy-path doc commit, which had no PR of its own.
>
> ## Checks
>
> - 324 passed, 1 skipped — full suite
> - 19 passed — the new guard, with the attach line removed by injection to confirm it fails
>

## #139 — Stop the route guard inventing work

- merged · opened 2026-07-30 · merged 2026-07-30
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/139>

> `clientpaths.py` is byte-identical across the three repos, so this carries a fix found in QRME. Companion PRs [qrme#231](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/qrme/pull/231) and [jim#178](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/178).
>
> ## The bug
>
> A template literal may nest another inside an interpolation:
>
> ```ts
> `/marketplace${tag ? `?tag=${enc(tag)}` : ""}`
> ```
>
> The backtick alternative was `` [^`]* ``, which stops at the **inner** opening backtick. The captured literal became `` /marketplace${tag ? ``, the query cut landed on the literal `?` inside it, and the call normalised to a path no route matches — so a route with a perfectly good client door was reported as having none.
>
> ## Why it is worth a commit here, where nothing changed
>
> **No PDI route was affected** — the count is 58 before and after, because no PDI call site uses that idiom yet. This lands because the file is shared, and because the *kind* of failure is the point.
>
> Every earlier defect in that file made the guard too **lenient**: a truncated path, a verb read off a neighbouring call, a route table read flat rather than recursed. Those get found by the bug they let through.
>
> This one made it **invent work**. Nothing failed, the suite stayed green, and a route sat on the backlog looking like work — a door-building round was aimed at it before anyone noticed the door was already there. A miss is found by its consequences; an invention is found only when somebody goes to do the work and finds it done.
>
> The distinction is now written into the file so the next reader knows both directions exist.
>
> ## The fix
>
> Interpolations are matched by counting braces rather than by a pattern that cannot span a nested one, and the optional-query marker recognises a backtick as well as `"` and `'` — the `${x ? "?a=b" : ""}` idiom is usually written with a nested template.
>
> ## Notes
>
> - No product changes; nothing touches the vault.
> - Route-guard tests green: 9 passed. Doorless backlog unchanged at 58, which is the expected result.
>
> 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

## #137 — Fail when a surface ships with no drawing, then draw the two that did

- merged · opened 2026-07-30 · merged 2026-07-30
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/137>

> Companion PRs [qrme](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/qrme/pulls) and [jim#176](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/176). Two commits: the guard, then the drawings it asks for.
>
> ## The guard
>
> The gallery tests all check screens against the README — a reference with no file, a file with no reference, a gap in the numbering, a stated count that drifted. Every one starts from the screens, and none asks the opposite question: **does this surface have a screen at all?** So a feature can ship with nothing drawn, nothing taught and nothing for the assistant to point at, and the suite stays green.
>
> That has happened three times across the suite, most recently in 0.19.0: the error-reporting card and its first-run notice shipped undrawn while the release notes described the feature at length.
>
> It is the same shape of flaw found twice elsewhere — **a guard that only walks the relation in the direction where the answers already exist.** The doorless audit was a route with no client door; the redaction check read a shrinking snapshot and would have gone vacuous the day it emptied.
>
> `ui_screens.txt` is the missing direction. Every console surface carries a screen number, `undrawn`, or `unaudited`.
>
> **Why the mapping is declared rather than inferred.** Matching component names against screen titles resolved only a third of them, because titles are written for the person using the app and component names for the person editing it. A fuzzy match would have produced a mapping that looked complete and was not — so `unaudited` is the honest seed for components that predate the file, and it is not a status a new component may use.
>
> **Both backlogs are ratcheted against a ceiling each repo declares for itself**, because this test is byte-identical across three repositories with different backlogs and one hardcoded number would be the largest of the three, leaving the other two slack. A ceiling left high after the backlog falls fails too — a ratchet that stops ratcheting re-opens the ground it gained.
>
> Verified by injection, five ways:
>
> ```
> unclassified surface                        -> fails
> silenced by writing `undrawn`               -> fails (the ratchet)
> mapping points at a screen that not exist   -> fails
> typo'd status ("undrwan")                   -> fails
> component deleted, entry left behind        -> fails
> raising the ceiling deliberately            -> passes, and shows in the diff
> ```
>
> ## The drawings
>
> Screens **46 What Went Wrong** and **47 Before Anything Is Sent**, with gallery rows, a lesson, and assistant phrasings for the words somebody actually types when something has broken.
>
> The card draws an operation and a status and nothing else, because that is all the log holds. It states plainly that **nothing here touches the vault** — no record, no key and no seal is involved, and a query string that could name a key never survives redaction.
>
> Both surfaces move off `undrawn`, and the ceiling drops to zero with them.
>
> ## One thing the existing guard caught
>
> The README stated **45 mobile screens against 47 on disk**. That check already existed — it is one of the directions this repo was checking — and it failed the moment the new screens landed, which is exactly the behaviour the new guard adds for the direction that was missing.
>
> ## Notes
>
> - No backend behaviour changes; nothing touches the vault.
> - Suite green: 305 passed, 1 skipped — up from 299.
>

## #135 — Send the error reports, and refuse anything that is not one

- merged · opened 2026-07-30 · merged 2026-07-30
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/135>

> The consoles have recorded content-free failures since pdi#134. This is the other half — they now **send** them, once at launch, alongside the update check. Companion PRs [qrme#227](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/qrme/pull/227) and [jim#174](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/174); `errors.ts`, `Problems.tsx`, `ProblemNotice.tsx` and the test are byte-identical across the three repos.
>
> ## Nothing goes before the person has been asked
>
> Sending is opt-**out**, which only means something if the opting-out can happen *before* the first report rather than being discovered afterwards in a settings panel nobody opened. A switch nobody knows about is not a choice.
>
> So `sendProblems` refuses until a first-run notice has been answered. That notice **renders the actual payload** rather than describing it, from the same function that posts it — prose saying "we only send error codes" would be a claim you have to trust; showing the object means the notice cannot go stale while still reading honestly. Both answers are offered, and it appears only where a build has a collector at all, because interrupting somebody to explain a thing that cannot happen is how people learn these notices are noise.
>
> Driven end to end against a live gateway:
>
> ```
> first launch, notice unanswered   -> awaiting-notice   (nothing sent)
> person clicks "No thanks"         -> turned-off        (nothing sent, ever)
> fresh install, before answering   -> awaiting-notice
>               after "That's fine" -> sent
> ```
>
> The aggregate then held exactly one row — from the install that agreed. The one that declined contributed nothing.
>
> After that first answer it is opt-out as normal: automatic, with the same switch on the Settings card, changeable whenever.
>
> ## Off by absence, not by flag
>
> The collector address is compiled in at build time and unset by default:
>
> ```bash
> PROBLEM_COLLECTOR=https://gw.example.com PROBLEM_TOKEN=… npm run build
> ```
>
> Unset, and the installer has nowhere to send and no code path that could acquire one — a stronger default than a boolean, because there is no address for a later mistake to switch on. The send swallows every failure; a diagnostic that can delay a launch has stopped being worth having.
>
> Sent from the renderer rather than the Electron main process, because that is where the buffer lives — a diagnostic needing an IPC channel of its own has more ways to go wrong than the bugs it finds. It lands beside the update check anyway: `setupAutoUpdate()` runs the moment the window is created.
>
> ## Counts are deltas
>
> Each row remembers how much of itself has been reported, so reopening the app twenty times does not turn one broken screen into twenty. Nothing is deleted after a send — the row is the user's own history. A failed send moves the watermark not at all:
>
> ```
> send to a dead collector -> failed,  watermark sent=0 of 1
> retry once it is up      -> sent,    watermark sent=1 of 1
> ```
>
> ## The gateway refuses rather than redacts
>
> `cloudgw/problems.py` (QRME's repository) accepts exactly five top-level keys and five per problem and **422s on anything else** — an unknown field, a `platform` string long enough to hide a sentence, a `day` carrying a time of day, a path with an unredacted id still in it.
>
> It could redact that path itself; the pattern is right there. It does not, because then a build whose redaction had broken would keep working and nobody would learn that every report from those users had been arriving with an id in it.
>
> What survives is **less than what arrives**: reports fold into counters keyed by product, version, platform, operation and status. Locale is validated and then dropped, and nothing records that a particular install sent anything, or when beyond the day. Reading that aggregate needs a narrower permission than writing to it — the posting token ships inside every installer and is public the moment somebody unzips one.
>
> ## Four bugs found by checking rather than reasoning
>
> **A gap in the guard itself.** Injecting a `detail` field into the outgoing report was caught — but only by the test comparing the wire shape against the gateway, which runs only in the repo that *ships* the gateway. In this repo and JIM it would have passed. The five field names are now pinned locally too.
>
> **Every validator was wrong about its own rule.** All the gateway's patterns were anchored with `$`, which in Python matches before a trailing newline as well as at end of string — so `Win32\n` was accepted by a validator whose error message said newlines were not allowed. All now end `\Z`, with a test.
>
> **No CORS at all** — the one that would have made the rest pointless. The sender posts JSON with an `authorization` header, making it a non-simple request: the browser preflights with `OPTIONS` and refuses the real call unless that is answered. Every preflight would have 405'd, every report would have failed, and because the sender swallows failures the feature would have been dead in the field with nothing to show for it. Found by asking what an Electron renderer's origin actually *is*: `null`, since it loads from `file://` — which is also why no origin allowlist could have been written.
>
> **A 500 on the read, found by being careless rather than clever.** While driving the client, a scratch file of unrelated JSON got reused as the counter path. The aggregate loaded it — it parsed — and reading the aggregate then died sorting values with no `count`. Unparseable JSON had been handled; *parseable* JSON of the wrong shape had not. Rows are validated individually on load now.
>
> ## Notes
>
> - No backend changes here; nothing touches the vault. No record, no key, no seal is involved.
> - Console typechecks and builds; suite green (299 passed, 1 skipped).
>
> 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

## #134 — Record what fails, without recording anything private

- merged · opened 2026-07-30 · merged 2026-07-30
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/134>

> Error collection across all three consoles, built so it cannot carry user data. Companion to [qrme#226](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/qrme/pull/226) and [jim-mini#173](https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/jim-mini/pull/173); `errors.ts`, `Problems.tsx` and the test are byte-identical across the three repos.
>
> ## For a vault, a key name is content
>
> Every failed request already passes through one function, so catching them is ~30 lines. The hard part is what a failure may say about itself — and in PDI the path itself is the risk. A request to read a record names the key, and a key name can say as much as the value:
>
> ```
> /bequests/grant/read?key=notes/private
> ```
>
> The query string never survives redaction, and identifying segments become `{id}` on the way in. What is recorded is the shape of the operation, never the thing operated on.
>
> | Recorded | Never recorded |
> |---|---|
> | operation (`GET /bequests/grant/read`) | the error message |
> | status (0 = never reached a server) | key names, prefixes, tenant ids |
> | count, date (day only) | query strings, request or response bodies |
> | app version, platform, language | grant tokens or customer keys |
>
> Redaction happens **on the way in**, so there is no moment at which the buffer holds something that would have to be scrubbed later.
>
> ## Nothing transmits
>
> Local, capped at 50, with a Settings card showing the exact payload — the same object the copy button produces, from one function, so the preview cannot drift from what is copied.
>
> **Nothing here touches the vault.** No record, no key and no seal is involved; the log holds route shapes and status codes.
>
> ## Two mistakes caught by testing, not by reasoning
>
> **The redaction under-redacted.** Requiring six hex characters let short ids through — three of the first eight real paths. Widened, then validated against 239 real route segments across the three products to confirm it does not eat route names in the other direction.
>
> **The test checking that had the same shape of flaw** — it read segments from `doorless_routes.txt`, which shrinks every time a door is built, so it would have weakened as the backlog cleared and gone vacuous the day it emptied. It now reads the live route table.
>
> ## Twelve tests hold the shape
>
> No parameter a message could arrive through, no field it could sit in, short ids caught as well as long, no real route name eaten. **Verified by injecting both leaks** and watching them fail.
>
> ## Notes
>
> - No backend changes. No new routes, so the doorless backlog stays at 58.
> - Console typechecks and builds; suite green at **293 passed**.
>

## #132 — Continuity finally has a door

- merged · opened 2026-07-30 · merged 2026-07-30
- `claude/pdi-cut-0.11.1` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/132>

> PDI's first pass at the doorless backlog, starting where it matters most for a vault.
>
> **Bequests are the whole continuity path** — what may be read, by whom, if a condition is ever attested. The backend was complete and no client opened it. For a vault that is the part that counts at exactly the moment the person who set it up is not present to help.
>
> ## Three actors, kept visibly apart
>
> Conflating them is how a continuity feature becomes a back door.
>
> | Actor | Credential | Act |
> |---|---|---|
> | **tenant** | tenant token | records a bequest, revokes it while alive |
> | **operator** | admin token | activates against an attested reference |
> | **heir** | grant token **+** customer key | reads what the bequest covers |
>
> The operator's credential is deliberately *not* the tenant's: the person who wrote the bequest cannot also be the one who declares its condition met. The activation reference goes into the audit chain, and the grant token is shown **once**.
>
> The heir needs **two separate secrets and neither works alone** — the grant token says the condition was attested, the customer key decrypts. This is enforced by the backend (`customer key must be base64` on a token-only attempt), and the screen states it rather than letting someone discover it.
>
> ## "Created" does not mean "in force"
>
> A bequest grants nothing when written. **Dormant**, **in force** and **revoked** are three different words on the card, because leaving "created" to imply "active" is the kind of ambiguity that matters only once, and badly.
>
> ## The suite gateway came with it
>
> Who is on shift and whether anybody is, the timezone those hours are read in, and the pages raised when nobody was reachable — each with whether it arrived, since a page that failed to deliver is the one worth seeing.
>
> The **ceiling** is rendered key by key from `GET /gate/ceiling` rather than paraphrased. Summarising it in the console would make the console the authority on a boundary it does not own. Its own sentence puts it best:
>
> > the agent's ceiling is whatever a wrong answer cannot undo
>
> ## Coverage
>
> **Fifteen routes off the doorless list, 73 → 58.**
>
> - bequests: create, list, revoke, activate, admin-revoke-grant (5)
> - heir side: grant keys, grant read (2)
> - gateway: ceiling, channel, roster get/add/remove, timezone, pages, retry (8)
>
> ## Notes
>
> - `req()` grew an optional `extra` headers field — the heir endpoints are the reason, since they authenticate with two secrets and neither is a bearer session. No change for existing callers.
> - Every shape read off a live response, including the full activate → redeem lifecycle.
> - Console typechecks and builds; full suite running at time of writing.
> - Nothing here changes what the vault does. A sealed record is sealed exactly as it was.
>
> 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
