## #46 — Run the PDI console from your phone: served console, pairing, installable PWA

- merged · opened 2026-07-24 · merged 2026-07-24
- `claude/pdi-mobile` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/46>

> ## What
>
> Completes the mobile round across the suite (jim-mini#67, qrme#99) — operate the vault's operator console from a phone with nothing to install and nothing to configure.
>
> **The API serves the console.** The built console mounts at `/app`, so the UI and the API share one origin — the phone loads the page and calls the address it came from. No CORS setup, no "which host is the backend?" step. Mounted last so it can never shadow an API route, and skipped entirely when `app/` hasn't been built (the API stays headless as before).
>
> **`GET /pair` finds the machine.** It resolves this machine's local-network address from the routing table and returns the URL to open, plus `GET /pair/qr.svg` as a scannable QR — both surfaced in a pairing card on the Settings screen. Loopback is treated as a failure, not a fallback (on a phone `127.0.0.1` means the phone), and every blocker — unbuilt console, unreachable address — is reported at once rather than one per round trip.
>
> **Installable as a PWA** — manifest, icon, standalone display, and a service worker that caches the **app shell only**, never API traffic: a stale sealed record or audit state shown as current is worse than an error.
>
> **Phone layout** — the sidebar becomes a thumb-reachable bottom tab bar, inputs sit at 16px so iOS doesn't zoom on focus, and the layout respects the notch and home indicator. Also fixes the app title, which said "QRME".
>
> ```bash
> npm --prefix app run build          # build the console once
> uvicorn pdi.api:app --host 0.0.0.0  # listen on the network
> curl localhost:8000/pair            # what to open on the phone
> ```
>
> Nothing changes in the security posture: the address is local-network only, admin endpoints still require `PDI_ADMIN_TOKEN`, and tenant data still requires the tenant's bearer token.
>
> ## Testing
>
> - `pdi/tests/test_mobile.py` (7): pairing returns a reachable address (never loopback) and a QR encoding it; the loopback case is reported honestly with the fix; both blockers report together; the console mounts at `/app` when built without shadowing the API; the API stays headless when it isn't; the shipped PWA declares itself installable and its worker leaves API traffic alone.
> - Full suite: **101 passed**, run with and without a built console.
> - **Live-server check against the LAN address**: `/app/`, manifest, service worker, icon, and QR all serve; tenant creation, sealing a record, reading it back, and `GET /audit/verify` (chain intact) all work from that origin.
>

## #44 — Terms of Service: B2B service terms, served versioned, receipted per tenant

- merged · opened 2026-07-24 · merged 2026-07-24
- `claude/pdi-terms` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/44>

> ## What
>
> The PDI slice of the terms round — B2B service terms with a per-tenant receipt:
>
> - **docs/terms.md (v1.0)** — frames PDI as what it is: encrypted data-custody **infrastructure, not advice** (compliance tooling supports the Customer's program but doesn't make them compliant). The Customer owns its data and answers for its lawfulness, consents, **tenant-token safekeeping** (the token is the tenant), and every connected system. **PHI requires the recorded BAA** — which the API already enforces in code, and which controls over these Terms where they conflict. Then: a release for the inherent risks of networked storage (carving out gross negligence and willful misconduct), an as-is warranty disclaimer, a liability cap (greater of 12-month fees or US $100), indemnification, suspension/termination with export during the recovery window, and a counsel placeholder for governing law. Marked as a template requiring counsel review.
> - **`GET /terms`** — public endpoint serving the version, key points, and document path.
> - **Per-tenant receipt** — provisioning a tenant stamps `terms_version` + `terms_accepted_at` on the tenant row, so every tenant carries proof of which agreement version was in force when it was created. (No clickwrap gate: tenants are admin-provisioned B2B — the BAA/service agreement is the signed instrument; this records the version that governs.)
>
> ## Testing
>
> - `pdi/tests/test_terms.py`: terms served versioned with the BAA and as-is key points; tenant provisioning records the version + timestamp receipt.
> - Full suite: **94 passed**.
>

## #43 — BAA enforcement: executed per customer before production PHI, in code

- merged · opened 2026-07-24 · merged 2026-07-24
- `claude/pdi-baa-enforcement` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/43>

> "Review the BAA template and execute it per customer before production PHI" stops being a checklist item and becomes **machine-enforced**.
>
> ## Enforcement (`pdi/baa.py`)
>
> The operator records each customer's executed BAA against their tenant — `POST /tenants/{id}/baa` (admin token) with parties, signatories, effective date, and the SHA-256 of the signed document (**metadata only**; the instrument stays with counsel). From then on the gate holds:
>
> - Any **HIPAA-program transfer or intake** for a tenant with no active BAA is **refused (403)** with a pointer at the template
> - Recording unblocks; `DELETE …/baa` terminates and re-blocks while keeping history (`executed → superseded/terminated`)
> - Tenants check their own standing at `GET /baa` (so JIM's pre-production checklist can verify it programmatically)
> - `baa.execute` / `baa.terminate` land in the tamper-evident audit chain
> - Non-HIPAA programs (OSHA, CPNI, …) are untouched
>
> ## Template review pass
>
> `docs/baa-template.md` gains the §164.530(f) **mitigation duty**, the customary **unsuccessful-attempts security-incident carve-out** (pings/scans/denied logins reported in aggregate), and a "Recording execution" section documenting the code-level gate. Enterprise guide and README updated to match.
>
> ## Tests
>
> `pdi/tests/test_baa.py` (4): HIPAA flows blocked until a BAA then unblocked with the execution in the audit chain; non-HIPAA programs never need one; termination re-blocks and keeps history + re-execution works; unknown tenants 404. Existing HIPAA-flow tests now execute a BAA first via the `new_tenant_with_baa` helper — the gate they document.
>
> **92 tests pass.**
>

>
> A production-ready Business Associate Agreement: permitted uses under the minimum-necessary standard, Security Rule safeguards, breach reporting fed by the audit chain, subcontractor flow-down, individual rights (access / amendment / accounting of disclosures), HHS availability, and return-or-destroy-on-termination via the verified-deletion path — plus **Exhibit B mapping every contractual promise to the concrete PDI control that keeps it** (AES-256-GCM + AAD tenant binding, hashed tokens, the hash-chained audit log, envelope key rotation, retention). Unsigned builds still succeed exactly as before.

- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/39>

> A **"Help us improve"** section now lives on the Overview screen on every client — send an idea, an improvement, a bug, or praise about PDI itself, with an optional 1–5 rating.
>
> This is **meta feedback on PDI as a product**, not tenant record data, so it sits outside the per-tenant namespace and needs no token. Feedback is **private**: a submitter sees only their own submissions plus the public tally by category, so the "you're heard" loop shows without exposing anyone's words. When a tenant token is presented the submitter is recorded so they can find their own submissions again; otherwise it's anonymous.

## #38 — Seed the starter vault: a demo tenant with sealed records to explore

- merged · opened 2026-07-24 · merged 2026-07-24
- `claude/pdi-starter-vault` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/38>

> ## Summary
>
> A fresh PDI deployment is an empty vault — every guarantee (AES-256-GCM at rest, AAD tenant+key binding, hash-chained audit) is real but invisible until something is sealed. This PR seeds the cold start, mirroring QRME's Starter Collection (qrme#71) and JIM's starter specialists (jim-mini#51):
>
> - **`pdi/seed.py`** — creates a `starter-demo` tenant with sample sealed records covering **every provenance origin**: a direct tenant write (`welcome/readme`, `finance/2025/summary`), a JIM Guardian deposit (`jim/demo-user/medical/checkin-001`), and a QRME deposit (`qrme/demo-profile/sources/note-001`). Also binds a NEO home robot, seals two collected items (map + sensor log), and reads one record back so the audit trail shows a full seal → access custody cycle from day one. Runnable as `python -m pdi.seed`.
> - **Token discipline preserved**: the tenant token is returned exactly once, by the run that creates the tenant — the same rule as `POST /tenants`. Re-seeding is idempotent and never re-issues (or could re-issue) the token, since only its hash is persisted.
> - **`pdi/api.py`** — `POST /seed`, admin-gated like the other control-plane routes.
>
> ## Tests
>
> `pdi/tests/test_starter_vault.py`: tenant creation with sealed record listing and decrypt read-back, idempotency without token re-issue or duplicate records, provenance origin per key prefix with an intact audit chain, bound robot with sealed collection data, and the seal→access audit cycle.
>
> **84 passed** (79 existing + 5 new).

## #37 — Language at the sign-in gateway, delivery modes, and dictionary translate

- merged · opened 2026-07-24 · merged 2026-07-24
- `claude/pdi-translate-gateway` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/37>

> PDI's version of the setup-gateway + translate round, scoped to what a vault honestly can do:
>
> ## 1. Language at the sign-in gateway
>
> All three native Welcome screens gain the language picker beside the token/server fields; the choice applies via `PUT /language` the moment the token validates, so the tenant's first vault response already carries translated notes.
>
> ## 2. Delivery mode
>
> The language preference carries a `mode`: **`pre`** (default — the middleware swaps translated notes into every JSON response) or **`on_demand`** (English notes kept). Sealed data is untouched either way. A "Pre-translate notes" toggle rides on every client's language card; flipping back to `pre` restores translated notes immediately.
>
> ## 3. `POST /translate` — dictionary-only by design
>
> PDI runs no model, so its translate endpoint is exact: known note strings translate via the hand dictionary (`engine: "hand"`); anything else returns the original with an honest note — *"PDI performs no machine translation — only its own note strings are translated"* — never a machine-mangled guess.
>
> ## Verification
>
> - 2 new tests: mode round-trip on live responses, and the dictionary/honesty/validation paths of `/translate`.
> - Full suite: **79 passed**. XAML parses clean; brace/paren balance passes.
>

## #35 — Per-tenant language + sealed-record provenance

- merged · opened 2026-07-24 · merged 2026-07-24
- `claude/pdi-language-provenance` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/35>

> Ports the language + provenance guarantees to PDI, mapped to what a vault actually is: no generated text, so localization is exact string substitution, and provenance is cryptographic proof of where data came from.
>
> ## Language (`/languages`, `GET/PUT /language`)
>
> PDI's responses are structured facts plus a small set of fixed explanatory notes ("robot data is encrypted at rest in the vault", …). Those notes are **hand-translated** (Spanish and French) and a response middleware swaps them in for any tenant who sets a language — anywhere they appear in a JSON response. Only exact known strings are touched: structured data and sealed values pass through byte-identical, and languages without translations keep English notes rather than machine-mangling them. Language changes are audited; all three native clients gain the picker on the Overview screen.
>
> ## Provenance (`GET /provenance/{key}`)
>
> A sealed record's verifiable derivation trail — proof, not trust:
>
> - **Origin**: JIM Guardian or QRME tandem writes (recognized by key prefix) vs. a direct API write by the tenant.
> - **Seal**: AES-256-GCM envelope encryption with the DEK stored only wrapped, and the AAD binding to *this tenant + key* — the ciphertext cannot be moved or re-attributed. Seal timestamps and ciphertext size included.
> - **Audit**: every event that ever touched the key (put/get/delete/…), plus the **hash-chain verification status** — `intact: true` means no audit entry has been altered or removed since it was written. Viewing provenance is itself an audited action.
>
> All three native clients gain a per-record **ⓘ Provenance** action on the Vault screen showing origin, cipher, binding, seal time, audit count, and chain state.
>
> ## Verification
>
> - 6 new tests: language catalog/choice/validation, note localization in live responses (es), English fallback for uncovered languages, the full provenance trail (origin, cipher, AAD, put/get audit events, intact chain, self-auditing), direct-write origin, and 404 on missing records.
> - Full suite: **75 passed**. XAML parses clean; brace/paren balance passes.
>

## #30 — Robots as vault-backed data sources (catalog, sealed ingest, custody)

- merged · opened 2026-07-23 · merged 2026-07-23
- `claude/pdi-robotics` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/30>

> ## Summary
> Same robotics catalog as QRME and JIM-mini (`pdi/robotics.py`). In PDI a bound robot is a **data source**: the maps, camera snapshots, and sensor logs it collects while working a home are exactly the kind of intimate data that must never sit in plaintext on a vendor cloud.
>
> ## Behavior
> - `POST /robots` (writer token) binds; `GET /robots` lists; `DELETE /robots/{rid}` unbinds — further ingest refused, sealed data stays tenant-owned
> - `POST /robots/{rid}/ingest` seals one item (`map | snapshot | sensor_log`) into the vault under `robot/{model}/{id}/{kind}/…` (AES-256-GCM at rest); every intake is hash-chained in the audit log so custody of what a robot saw is provable
> - `GET /robots/{rid}/data` lists the robot's vault keys; values are read through the normal audited `GET /records/{key}` path
> - Tenant isolation enforced: another tenant cannot see or feed your robot
>
> ## Verification
> 7 new tests (catalog, sealed ingest + audit chain intact, per-robot key scoping, kind/model validation, unbind semantics, tenant isolation, auth); **full suite 69 passed**. Screen 36 (Robot Data Vault) rendered and verified.

## #29 — Add native iOS/Android/Windows apps for PDI Vault

- merged · opened 2026-07-23 · merged 2026-07-23
- `claude/pdi-native` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/29>

> ## Summary
> Scaffolds three idiomatic **native** clients for PDI under a new `native/` directory, mirroring the JIM-mini native structure. Each is a separate native codebase wired to the real PDI backend with **tenant token authentication** — no backend changes.
>
> | Platform | Stack | Folder |
> |---|---|---|
> | iOS | Swift + SwiftUI (XcodeGen) | `native/ios/` |
> | Android | Kotlin + Jetpack Compose (Gradle) | `native/android/` |
> | Windows | C# + WinUI 3 (Windows App SDK) | `native/windows/` |
>
> ## Auth model & screens
> PDI is multi-tenant and does not self-register: the tenant is issued a `pdi_…` bearer token out of band and pastes it to unlock. Every call carries `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
>
> - **Sign in** → paste token, validated via `GET /records` (200 = valid), persisted
> - **Overview** → record count (`GET /records`) + integrity (`GET /audit/verify`) + masked token
> - **Vault** → `PUT /records` to seal, `GET /records/{key}` to reveal, `DELETE /records/{key}`
> - **Audit** → `GET /audit/verify` badge + `GET /audit` entry list
>
> Host defaults: `127.0.0.1:8000` (iOS/Windows), `10.0.2.2:8000` (Android emulator); base URL entered at sign-in.
>
> ## Verification
> Static checks only (no native toolchain on Linux CI): all XAML/XML well-formed, `project.yml` valid YAML, braces/parens balanced across every `.swift`/`.kt`/`.cs`, Android `namespace`/`applicationId`/manifest consistent (`com.pdi.vault`). `python3 -m pytest -q` → **62 passed** (backend untouched).
>

## #25 — Inbound compliant intake: subscribers & partner companies send files IN

- merged · opened 2026-07-23 · merged 2026-07-23
- `claude/pdi-intake-transfers` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/25>

> ## Summary
>
> Completes the enterprise transfer story in **both directions**, so PDI carries files *for broadband users **and** companies*.
>
> Outbound (already shipped) seals a file *out* to a recipient. **Intake** is the reverse: a corporation requests a file *in* from a broadband **subscriber** or a **partner company**, and that party submits it safely — sealed in the vault under the same compliance controls (HIPAA / OSHA / CPNI / …), with a one-shot **submit token** and full chain of custody.
>
> ## Endpoints
>
> | Method | Path | Purpose |
> |--------|------|---------|
> | POST | `/intakes` | request a file from a party under program(s) → submit token |
> | GET | `/intakes` · `/intakes/{id}` | list / one |
> | POST | `/intakes/{id}/submit` | subscriber/partner sends the file in (`X-Submit-Token`, no tenant credential) |
> | GET | `/intakes/{id}/file` | the corporation retrieves it (audited) |
> | GET | `/intakes/{id}/custody` | requested → submitted → read, audit chain attested |
> | DELETE | `/intakes/{id}` | close |
>
> Every transfer and intake is now tagged with a **`party_type`** (`subscriber` \| `organization` \| `partner`), so the record shows whether it served a broadband user or a company.
>
> ## Also
>
> - New `pdi/intakes.py`, `intakes` table, `IntakeCreate`/`IntakeSubmit` models, and the enterprise doc extended with the intake flow + curl examples.
>
> ## Tests
>
> 5 new tests — subscriber submits & corporation reads, partner-company intake + custody (requested→submitted→read, chain intact), bad token / unknown program / resubmit closed, tenant isolation, transfer `party_type`. **Full suite 62 passing** (was 57).
>

## #24 — Enterprise compliance-grade secure file transfer (HIPAA, OSHA, CPNI, …)

- merged · opened 2026-07-23 · merged 2026-07-23
- `claude/pdi-enterprise-transfers` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/24>

> ## Summary
>
> PDI as a **private data infrastructure corporations run to move files safely for users under regulatory compliance** — carriers (AT&T, Verizon, T-Mobile), broadband, healthcare, financial, and public-sector operators.
>
> A tenant seals a file for a recipient under one or more compliance programs; PDI's core guarantees carry the regulated controls:
>
> - **Encrypted at rest** — sealed in the vault (AES-256-GCM, AAD-bound to tenant + key).
> - **Audit-logged access** — creation and every retrieval land in the tamper-evident hash-chained log and the transfer's chain of custody; the custody record attests the chain is intact.
> - **Scoped retrieval** — a one-shot **receive token** (only its SHA-256 stored) authorizes the recipient; no tenant credential needed.
> - **Enforced retention** — the strictest window across its programs (OSHA 5y, HIPAA 6y, SOX 7y…); revoke cuts access but keeps the record through retention.
>
> ## Compliance catalog
>
> `GET /compliance/programs` — **HIPAA · HITECH · OSHA · CPNI · SOC 2 · PCI-DSS · GDPR · CCPA/CPRA · GLBA · SOX · FERPA · FedRAMP · CJIS**, each mapping required controls to what PDI satisfies natively vs what stays operational (BAA, consent, breach-notification workflow).
>
> ## Endpoints
>

## #20 — Social connectors: collect into the vault, publish/run via QR beacons

- merged · opened 2026-07-22 · merged 2026-07-22
- `claude/pdi-social-connectors` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/20>

> ## Summary
>
> A social-platform **connector** layer for PDI, fitting its role as the storage layer:
>
> - **collect** — pulls the account's content *in* and seals each item as a **vault record** (`social/<platform>/<connector>/<id>`): encrypted at rest, AAD-bound to the tenant, and covered by the same audit chain and retention as every other record. This is where the raw social data other systems build profiles from actually lands.
> - **publish** — shares an update *on* the platform, reachable by a **QR beacon** (`segno`).
>
> Everything is **tenant-scoped** — a connector is only visible and usable under the token of the tenant that owns it.
>
> ## Endpoints
>
> | Method | Path | Purpose |
> |--------|------|---------|
> | POST | `/connectors` | link a platform (writer token) |
> | GET | `/connectors` | list this tenant's connectors |
> | DELETE | `/connectors/{cid}` | revoke |
> | POST | `/connectors/{cid}/ingest` | seal collected items as vault records |
> | POST | `/connectors/{cid}/publish` | share an update |
> | GET | `/connectors/{cid}/beacon` | presence URL + QR path |
> | GET | `/connectors/{cid}/qr.svg` | the QR beacon |
>
> ## Also
>
> - `connectors` table, `ConnectorCreate` / `ConnectorIngest` / `ConnectorPublish` models, `segno` dependency, and **Screen 28 · Connectors** (iOS + Android).

## #15 — Per-platform code signing (macOS vs Windows certs independent)

- merged · opened 2026-07-22 · merged 2026-07-22
- `claude/per-os-signing` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/15>

> ## Summary
>
> The signed build fed the **same** `CSC_LINK` to every OS, so adding only a macOS certificate would make the **Windows** job try to sign with the Apple `.p12` and fail the whole release. Make signing **per-platform and opt-in**: macOS uses `CSC_LINK`, Windows uses `WIN_CSC_LINK`, Linux never signs. Each platform sees only its own cert. Docs updated.
>

## #13 — Fix desktop-release packaging (electron-builder)

- merged · opened 2026-07-22 · merged 2026-07-22
- `claude/fix-desktop-packaging` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/13>

> ## Summary
>
> Mirrors the QRME packaging fix, surfaced by a dry run of `desktop-release.yml`:
>
> 1. **Empty `CSC_LINK` broke the build** — electron-builder treats an empty-string cert path as a file and fails. Split into a **signed** build (cert secret present) and an explicitly **unsigned** build (`CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false`).
> 2. **Missing package metadata** made `computeChannelNames` throw `Cannot read properties of null (reading 'channel')`. Added `author` + `repository` and set `build.publish: null`.
>
> Installers package unsigned by default, signed when secrets are configured. Renderer build unchanged.
>

## #9 — Add PDI Position & Assistant Builder (AI Integration & Role-Mapping)

- merged · opened 2026-07-21 · merged 2026-07-21
- `claude/pdi-positions` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/9>

> ## Summary
>
> Delivers the **AI Integration & Role-Mapping Questionnaire** as a PDI service: an operator answers a short, **industry-agnostic** questionnaire about a role and PDI returns a personalized **assistant blueprint** — recommended capabilities, an automation-opportunity score, human-in-the-loop guardrails, reskilling paths, and a ready-to-use assistant system-prompt.
>
> Works for any sector (transit, healthcare, logistics, hospitality, finance, manufacturing, public administration…) — the operator types their own industry and the builder never assumes one.
>
> ## Responsible by design
>
> This is **decision support, not an automated staffing decision.**
>
> - High-stakes decisions — incident response, contracts, budgets, staffing, safety/regulatory sign-off — are always flagged **human-in-the-loop**, regardless of the automation score.
> - Obsolescence is framed as **tasks to automate**, never a verdict on a person.
> - **Reskilling / repositioning paths** are surfaced alongside.
> - The generated assistant spec instructs the sub-model to *surface options and defer final judgement to the human it assists* — it suggests, never finalizes.
>
> ## Privacy
>
> Raw questionnaire answers are sensitive workforce data. They're sealed with AES-256-GCM under `positions/{id}` in the tenant's vault — the same envelope encryption, key rotation, retention, and tamper-evident audit chain that protect every other PDI record. Only the derived blueprint leaves the vault.
>
> ## Changes
>
> - **`pdi/positions.py`** — questionnaire → blueprint logic (capabilities with reasons, explainable automation score, human-in-loop mapping, reskilling, guardrailed assistant system-prompt).
> - **`pdi/models.py`** — `PositionIntake` with 7 optional sections mirroring the questionnaire; partial intake still yields a safe defaulted blueprint.
> - **`pdi/api.py`** — `POST /positions` (seal raw answers + return blueprint, write-scoped), `GET /positions`, `GET /positions/{id}`.
> - **`pdi/audit.py`** — `position.create` audit action.
> - **`app/`** — Positions console screen (renders the questionnaire + blueprint: capability list, automation meter, human-in-loop, reskilling, assistant prompt), typed API client methods, wired into nav.
> - **`docs/positions.md`** + README (feature list + API table).
> - **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`** — front-end smoke-build job so every PR proves the console still type-checks and builds.
>
> ## Testing
>
> - `pytest pdi/tests` — **38 passed** (5 new: blueprint framing, industry-agnostic/partial intake, seal + return + list + fetch, read-only token rejection, 404).
> - `npm run build` (console) — clean type-check + Vite build.
>

## #7 — Production key management, retention up to forever, audit event schema

- merged · opened 2026-07-21 · merged 2026-07-21
- `claude/pdi-keymgmt-retention` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/7>

> ## What this implements
>
> The concrete backend asks: **production key management, a full audit event schema, retention up to forever**, and a hardened cloud-model contribution intake. Real, tested code.
>
> ### Key management — envelope encryption + rotation (`pdi/crypto.py`)
> - A **key-encryption key (KEK)** never touches record data. Each **key version** owns a random **data-encryption key (DEK)** stored only *wrapped* by the KEK — the DB never holds usable key material.
> - **Rotation** mints a new version and re-seals records under it; old versions stay readable until retired.
> - Pluggable **key provider**: KEK in env (dev) or a **KMS/HSM** in production (`PDI_KEY_PROVIDER=kms`) — a loud integration seam, never a silent local fallback.
> - New: `POST /keys/rotate` (re-seals by default), `GET /keys`, `POST /keys/reseal`, `POST /keys/retire`.
>
> ### Retention — from a short window up to **forever** (`pdi/retention.py`)
> - Per-tenant record retention (`7d`/`30d`/`90d`/`180d`/`1y`/`forever`, default **forever**) + a global soft-delete recovery window (`PDI_RECOVERY_WINDOW`, default forever).
> - `POST /retention/sweep` expires records and purges tombstoned tenants past their window; `forever` expires nothing. The **audit chain is always kept forever** (pruning breaks tamper-evidence).
> - New: `GET /retention`, `PUT /tenants/{id}/retention`, `retention` on tenant create.
>
> ### Audit event schema (`pdi/audit.py`)
> - `GET /audit/schema` returns field definitions + the full action catalogue (category + meaning per action); every entry now carries a derived `category`.
>
> ### Contribution intake, hardened
> - Carries an anonymous `ref` and is revocable by it: `DELETE /contributions/{ref}`.
>
> ## Compatibility & tests
> - Sealed format is now `"<version>:base64(…)"`; **legacy blobs still decrypt** and upgrade on the next write/reseal.
> - **10 new tests, 33 total pass.** Boot-verified: rotate + reseal + read-through, retention forever, 19-action schema.
>
> 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
>

## #1 — Hash tenant bearer tokens at rest; constant-time admin compare

- merged · opened 2026-07-19 · merged 2026-07-19
- `claude/pdi-token-hashing` → `main`
- Author: davidsbianchi1984
- Page: <https://github.com/davidsbianchi1984/pdi/pull/1>

> ## Summary
>
> PDI already had solid access control (tenant tokens, read/write RBAC, admin token, instant revocation). This closes the one gap inconsistent with its purpose: for a product whose whole job is protecting data at rest, its **own bearer tokens were stored in plaintext** — a leak of PDI's database would hand an attacker working tenant credentials that unlock the vault. QRME and JIM already hash their tokens; PDI now matches.
>
> - `pdi/vault.py` — tenant tokens and scoped read/write tokens are stored as their **SHA-256 hash**. The plaintext is generated and returned exactly once at issuance; `tenant_by_token` and `revoke_token` hash the presented token before matching. A resolved-tenant object no longer carries the `token` field at all (not even the hash).
> - `pdi/api.py` — the `PDI_ADMIN_TOKEN` check now uses `secrets.compare_digest`, so a wrong admin token can't be recovered by timing.
> - `pdi/db.py` — schema comments updated to note the token columns hold hashes.
>
> No API surface change: issuance still returns the plaintext once; all existing flows are unchanged.
>
> ## Testing
>
> - New `pdi/tests/test_token_hashing.py` (3 tests): the DB stores the hash not the plaintext (primary + scoped tokens), and resolved tenants expose no token material.
> - Existing RBAC/revocation tests exercise the full issue → resolve → revoke path against the hashed store.
> - `python3 -m pytest pdi/tests -q` → **23 passed**.
>
> ## Docs
>
> README security section notes tokens are hashed at rest and the admin compare is constant-time.
>
